- [x] Compile and test in Mezzano, including the existing interruption suite.
- [x] Expose availability and test recovery through Chromium.
- [x] Update the reference, review, commit, and push.

## Interrupted frameless function omitted from backtrace: 2026-10-13

The new debugger-command interruption test passed.  The existing evaluation-interruption suite then failed its assertion that the WORK frame appears in the returned backtrace.  Retained test runner stop288 and worker stop287 were inspected.  The worker was interrupted in MEZZANO.RUNTIME::CHECK-SYMBOL-VALUE-TYPE at offset101.  GC metadata at that exact PC reports frame-p NIL, interrupt-p NIL, and layout-length1.  Reading the return address from the saved RSP identifies LISPM-INTERRUPT-TEST::WORK.  The current %MAP-BACKTRACE follows only saved RBP links and omits that caller when the interrupted function is frameless.  This identifies a debugger stack-walking defect.  No timing adjustment or weakened assertion is appropriate.

Recommendation for approval: represent captured debugger frames with explicit instruction, stack, and frame pointers and saved-register locations, and use the existing GC metadata rules to traverse framed, frameless, and interrupt activations.  Reuse and review BACKTRACE-NEXT-FRAME in system/lldb.lisp.  Preserve exact PCs for interrupted instructions and distinguish them from call return addresses.  Locals must use the matching frame base and saved registers.  Tests must force interruption in a frameless callee and verify the callee, caller, caller locals, GC, continuation, and nested interruption.  Existing frame/local and interruption tests remain required.  This representation correction precedes further frame-evaluation work.

Inspection mistakes: the first helper attempted to attach the worker while the suspended test retained its controller and correctly received :ALREADY-CONTROLLED.  Read the already accepted command result instead.  That read initially used the HTTP-facing name COMMAND-RESULT against the direct SWANK API, whose operation is RESULT.  Corrected the operation name.  Both errors were in the inspection driver.

The service, evaluation service, and new interruption fixture compiled with warnings NIL and failure NIL.  TEST-DEBUGGER-INTERRUPT:RUN passed printer continuation, reader abort and retry, nested interrupt restoration, pending-request rejection/replay, inhibited delivery, and exact cleanup counts.  The existing control fixture also passed.  The existing interruption fixture then exposed the frameless-caller defect described above, so the combined interruption increment remains uncommitted.  Evidence: debugger-interrupt-load-test.log, debugger-interrupt-existing.log, and debugger-missing-work-frame.json in the build directory.  The failed runner and worker, stops287/288, and their session were released after saving the frame evidence.

A captured code location must survive moving GC.  The proposed frame representation will retain a function object with an instruction offset, or the address of the saved PC slot, rather than an untracked copy of a code address.  Saved-register locations must identify the live saved state, and locals must distinguish register contents from register-slot addresses.  The current high-level frame structure only contains a frame-pointer link and copied callee-save register values, which cannot describe the missing frameless caller.  The exact representation and caller reconstruction remain subject to the requested approval and tests.

## Reuse of the existing stack walker: 2026-10-13

The user rejected the broad frame-state proposal and authorized reviewing and implementing the smallest adapter to the existing walker.  BACKTRACE-NEXT-FRAME already distinguishes framed calls, frameless calls, and interrupt frames from GC metadata.  Extend its returned values with the address of the saved PC, the saved-frame-link address when present, and whether the PC is an interrupted instruction.  Existing callers continue using its first three values.

MAP-BACKTRACE can then keep its current frame-pointer-link representation, retaining that link across frameless calls.  Each frame needs the saved PC slot and the interrupted-PC flag.  The stack pointer remains a traversal variable.  Reading the saved PC slot follows GC's relocation of code references.  FUNCTION-FROM-FRAME uses that slot and distinguishes an interrupted instruction from a call return address.  Stack-slot access must report unavailable when GC metadata says the function has no established frame.  This adaptation preserves the existing frame-pointer meaning for internal binding-write clients and requires no copied register state.

Required tests retain the missing-caller assertion and add a deterministic frameless callee with a debug-3 caller: verify both functions, caller locals, full GC, and successful continuation.  Review and rerun existing frame/local, binding-write, debugger-control, and interruption tests.  The broader frame-evaluation agenda remains separate.

## Nested input wait accounting: 2026-10-13

Chromium testing found that aborting an interactive restart reader clears INPUT-WAITING-P while the original interrupted READ-LINE still waits on the same stream.  STREAM-READ-CHAR sets and clears one Boolean for each dynamic reader.  The nested reader therefore erases the outer reader's state.  Supplying input completed the original evaluation, confirming that its read remained active.

Recommendation: count registered waiting reads under the existing service lock.  Each read increments only when it must wait and decrements its own registration during unwind cleanup.  INPUT-WAITING-P reports whether that count is positive.  No protocol field changes.  Test an outer READ-LINE, interrupt, nested interactive restart read, second interrupt, command Abort, original CONTINUE, visible input wait, supplied line, and exact cleanup counts.  Check for live waiting readers before replacing the class and method.  The Web agent will rerun its corresponding browser interaction afterward.

## Snapshot failure and thread retention: 2026-10-13

The final save failed after the debugger guest and Chromium tests passed.  Snapshot preparation completed, but a major collection during page writeback panicked with Insufficient space for garbage collection.  The disk header had not yet been updated.  The development guest stopped answering SWANK.  Paused QEMU PID 475778 and preserved the image, serial log, and a host core containing guest RAM under debugger-snapshot-panic-20261013 names.  The original VM remains untouched.

Read-only inspection of the preserved RAM found 621579 free backing-store blocks, reservation counter 588262, and 457566 mapped pages requiring reservations.  All current mapped pages are uncommitted after snapshot preparation.  Ordinary thread-stack regions account for 355760 mapped pages.  These measurements identify retained stack capacity and a separate reservation excess.  They do not yet establish the complete cause of the panic.

Source review found a definite SWANK thread-retention error.  SWANK/MEZZANO::*THREAD-IDS-FOR-EMACS* holds both thread-to-integer and integer-to-thread entries with weakness :KEY-OR-VALUE.  Mezzano's UPDATE-ONE-WEAK-POINTER retains such an entry whenever either member is live.  Immediate integer IDs are always live, so each entry retains its thread.  Recommendation: use :KEY-AND-VALUE, which requires the thread to remain independently reachable.  Preserve existing IDs when replacing the live table.  Test both directions, stable IDs across full collection for a live thread, and collection of an otherwise unreachable completed thread and its stack.  No new dependency is needed.

Recovery will restart the development guest from the last completed snapshot after preserving the failed state.  Restored debugger jobs can predate their latest commands.  Inspect surviving stops before performing any operation on them.  Reload the compiled debugger changes after recovery, test the retention correction, reconcile backing-store reservations, and require successful snapshot and restore tests before claiming persistence works.

- [x] Explain and correct the reservation discrepancy.

## Dead-thread global-list links: 2026-10-13

Source review found that THREAD-FINAL-CLEANUP repairs neighboring list links and the global head but leaves THREAD-GLOBAL-NEXT and THREAD-GLOBAL-PREV on the removed object.  Thread objects use the ordinary instance pointer layout, so the collector follows these slots even when SCAN-THREAD skips the dead thread's stack contents.  A retained dead thread can therefore retain a former neighbor after that neighbor exits.

Recommendation: clear both global-list links after removal while the existing global thread lock is held.  Preserve the thread's own stack and join result for existing inspection and join callers.  Test two adjacent threads, retain the newer thread after it exits, then let the older thread exit.  Before the correction, require the retained thread's next link and a weak pointer to identify the older thread after full GC.  After the correction, require NIL links, collection of the older thread and its stack, stable joins for the retained thread, and intact live-list forward/backward links.  Compile the kernel function with wired allocation and test in Mezzano.  Existing dead objects may still contain links created by the previous implementation.

The first stale-link test panicked in CHECK-LIVE-LIST with Interrupts enabled when they shouldn't be.  The test called WITH-GLOBAL-THREAD-LOCK without disabling interrupts, violating that internal spinlock's calling requirements.  Replaced that direct lock access with the existing ALL-THREADS API.  The revised check verifies membership, absence of dead fixtures, and duplicate-free traversal.  It does not inspect backward links.  No thread-cleanup implementation change had been loaded.  Preserved the console as debugger-thread-test-panic-20261013.log.  The concurrent broad browser run lost its connection at this panic and must rerun after recovery.

Review of every global-list reader found that ALL-THREADS traverses the links without locking.  Clearing a removed thread's next link could truncate a concurrent traversal.  Extend the correction to capture the list into a temporary wired vector under the existing global lock with interrupts disabled, then construct the returned list after releasing the lock.  Allocate and grow that vector before locking.  Retry if concurrent creation exceeds its capacity.  This avoids allocation and pageable storage under the spinlock.  GC traversals already stop ordinary threads, the profiler suspends peer CPUs, and panic traversal is diagnostic.  Test ALL-THREADS during repeated creation and exit, including a list larger than the initial vector capacity.

The corrected test reproduced retention through THREAD-GLOBAL-NEXT after the neighboring thread exited and full GC completed.  Both changed kernel functions compiled at safety 3/debug 1/speed 1 with warnings NIL and failure NIL and loaded into the wired function area.  The positive test then observed NIL links on the retained dead thread, collection of its former neighbor and that neighbor's stack, and unchanged repeated join results.  Enumeration passed with 100 simultaneous waiting threads, exercising vector growth, while another thread created and joined 100 short-lived threads.  Each captured list contained every waiting fixture and the enumerating thread without duplicates.  Fixture cleanup completed.  Evidence: thread-unlink-recovery-before.log, thread-cleanup-load.log, and thread-unlink-after.log.  Existing dead objects from the recovered image have not been rewritten.

## Page reservation initialization and wired snapshots: 2026-10-13

Recommendation: initialize writable-store page reservations by traversing the current block map.  Each lazy, uncommitted, or wired page needs one reserved block.  Preserve the existing 256-block metadata allowance.  Read-only and freestanding initialization remain unchanged.  The current initialization counts every allocated disk block, including obsolete metadata, as a page requiring another block.

Snapshot preparation currently decrements reservations when copying wired area/function/card pages, then increments them while uncommitting those pages.  Wired stacks are excluded from that copy, so uncommitting a committed wired stack adds a reservation without a preceding decrement.  Keep the wired-page reservation throughout copying and do not increment it when uncommitting a wired page.  The net reservation remains one for each wired page.

Implement one counting function, use it at writable-store boot, and test against the independent read-only audit.  Reconcile the current counter once from that same state after loading the corrected definitions.  This removes reservations unsupported by the block map without releasing any disk block.  Require full-GC, repeated snapshot, and restore checks.  Releasing obsolete snapshot metadata and diagnosing the preserved failed restore remain separate work.

The four changed kernel functions compiled at safety 3/debug 1/speed 1 with warnings NIL and failure NIL and loaded into the wired function area.  The independent block-map audit agreed with COUNT-BLOCK-RESERVATIONS.  Initialized the live counter from 414442 to 284194, removing 130248 excess reservations while preserving the 256-block allowance.  The first test then stopped because its source used the external package spelling MEZZANO.INTERNALS:GC for the internal GC symbol.  Inspected the retained reader condition, aborted and released the owned job, and corrected the test to use MEZZANO.INTERNALS::GC.  No snapshot had started.

The compiled accounting fixture passed full GC and two completed snapshots.  Snapshot 1 reported 180312 page reservations before saving and 357606 afterward, each with the 256-block allowance.  Snapshot 2 reported 168848 before and 367519 afterward, again without accounting drift.  Saved the pretest image as debugger-reservation-before-20261013.image.  Prepared a retained debugger stop, timed synchronization waiters, and a browser session before restarting QEMU.

## Remote filesystem route availability after restore: 2026-10-13

The restored guest executed the new paging initializer, then panicked with Serial read char not implemented.  The failing SWANK accept thread was in AUTHENTICATE-CLIENT -> SLIME-SECRET -> remote OPEN -> TCP-CONNECT -> IPV4-ROUTE.  Its error was No route to host 10.0.2.2, before DHCP installed the replacement interface's routes.  The native debugger attempted input on its output-only serial stream.  Preserved the image, serial log, and QEMU memory under page-reservation-restore-panic names.  The browser restore assertions did not run.

The first description suggested a newly accepted connection.  Read-only inspection of the paused VM found that the stream in ACCEPT-CONNECTIONS refers to a connection from the previous boot: its boot event is signalled, its state is CLOSED, and its pending error is CONNECTION-STALE.  This distinguishes a restored authentication activation from evidence of a newly accepted connection.  TCP-ACCEPT also lacks stale-connection filtering, but changing that function alone cannot protect an authentication activation already saved after acceptance.

Recommendation: add an explicit WAIT-FOR-ROUTE option to TCP-CONNECT and TCP-STREAM-CONNECT.  When requested, wait on a condition variable associated with the existing route-table mutex until a route is available or the existing connection deadline expires.  ADD-ROUTE notifies waiters under that mutex.  Route waiting, DNS, and TCP establishment share the existing deadline.  Enable this option for remote filesystem connections, including connections without reuse.  Other TCP callers retain immediate NO-ROUTE-TO-HOST behavior.  No polling, fixed startup delay, disabled authentication, or replacement transport is required.

Tests must cover route arrival, deadline expiration without early return, interruption cleanup, immediate errors when the option is absent, and connection establishment with the option.  Retest existing network deadline and stream tests, then repeat snapshot restoration with concurrent browser reconnects and verify the retained debugger stop, locals, restart, synchronization waiters, page accounting, Listener, Document Examiner, and Peek.  Route removal during an established connection and hostname resolution before a DNS route exists remain separate cases.  Native background/startup error retention remains part of the debugger plan.

Restarting the same saved image after closing the test browser reproduced the same restored authentication failure.  This retry was diagnostic and did not change startup ordering.  Restored the previously boot-tested recovery image to compile the route-wait correction.  The failed snapshot and memory remain preserved.  The controlled route-arrival test will temporarily remove the guest's default route while retaining its host and loopback routes, restore it in unwind cleanup, and use only test-owned waiting threads.

The selective recovery loader first stopped while reading TCP source because it called SYMBOL-NAME on a SETF function name.  Inspected the retained type error and added a SYMBOLP check to the loader's selection predicate.  This is a host-generated loading fixture error.  The kernel and thread fixes had loaded, but the route-wait functions had not yet compiled.  The recovery image rejected the newer :ABORT operation with :UNKNOWN-OPERATION.  Queried its restarts, invoked the advertised ABORT through :INVOKE-RESTART, and released the owned stop, job, and session.  The remaining module load had already started after the cleanup command failed.

The route-wait functions compiled at safety 3/debug 1/speed 1 with warnings NIL and failure NIL.  The guest test passed route notification, a 1/20-second deadline with an elapsed-time lower bound, foothold cancellation and cleanup, immediate NO-ROUTE-TO-HOST when waiting was not requested, and loopback octet transfer when waiting was requested.  Existing DNS/TCP deadline, canceled queued start, connection refusal, interrupted connect, EOF, and abort-close tests passed.  Evidence: network-route-wait-test.log.

LOAD-INIT-FILE currently sleeps 0.5 seconds to let networking settle.  That delay cannot establish route availability.  Remove it now that remote filesystem connections wait for a route under the connection deadline, and include initialization without that delay in the restore test.

The initializer loading fixture used CL-USER instead of the source's MEZZANO.INTERNALS package and stopped on the package-local nickname SYS.INT.  Inspected that reader condition and corrected the fixture's IN-PACKAGE form.  The restore fixtures had prepared successfully in a separate job.  The failed initializer job was then aborted and released before recompiling it.

## Reservation test paging failure: 2026-10-13

The route-wait image restored past DHCP and the preceding authentication failure.  The first accounting probe then stopped answering.  Read-only guest memory inspection identified a wait cycle: the VM lock has one reader and a waiting writer, the pager waits in WAIT-FOR-PAGE for that lock, and a SWANK worker waits for a page while executing LISPM-PAGE-RESERVATION-TEST:CHECK.  Its saved PC is 0x8001b319e6, an indirect call through R13 = 0x8001b7e9a9.  The fault address is 0x8001b7e9a8, the cleanup function's entry-point slot.  The test acquired the VM lock in pageable code and could fault before releasing it.  Earlier tests passed while those pages happened to be resident.  This defect is in the newly added test.

Recommendation: add a wired kernel BLOCK-RESERVATION-STATISTICS function that acquires the VM read lock, reads the three counters, and releases the lock before returning.  CHECK calls that function and performs its assertions and formatting after return.  This keeps all code and data accessed under the VM lock resident without requiring callers to load their test or application into wired memory.  Preserve the failed guest's memory, restart the saved image, load this correction before running the accounting probe, and repeat the restore tests.  Do not modify guest locks or page tables from the host.

The temporary memory decoder initially assumed that cold map entries still named current compiled functions and supported only simple strings.  Following current function references and recognizing the observed string representation corrected those diagnostic errors.  The inspection did not write guest memory.

After restarting the same saved image, the wired reader and corrected test compiled with warnings NIL and failure NIL.  CHECK returned an assertion failure instead of blocking the pager: 378711 mapped reservations, counter 378966, expected 378967.  Inspected that condition through the retained debugger and aborted and released owned stop162 and its job.  The one-page deficit remains under investigation.  No counter adjustment was made.

Independent restore tests passed: expired handles and controller, retained command outcome, caller locals after full GC, successful restart, exact cleanup, timed condition-variable notification and timeout, and mutex reacquisition.  The statistics still differed by one page after full GC.  Evidence: reservation-reader-repair.log and route-restore-debugger-sync.log.

The broad Chromium suite and all nine focused debugger groups passed in the restored guest.  Their owned jobs, stops, and sessions were released.  The browser restoration driver had already reached its 15-minute deadline before guest recovery, so its restore assertions remain incomplete.  Evidence: /tmp/debugger-web-postrestore-ui-evidence.tar.gz on the browser host, SHA256 8ca5c2d5aef72e468f0d36164fd903023f15884c633a0140fdc7107ec89e5da1.  The route-wait correction passed its compiled guest tests and two boots from the saved image without the prior authentication route failure.  Page accounting remains a separate uncommitted increment.

The reservation deficit stays at one page through guest compilation, full GC, and the browser suites.  A wired audit found no currently mapped wired block whose physical frame had a pageable type.  This excludes that suspected current-state mismatch but does not explain the deficit.

Next diagnostic: instrument individual block-map transitions in temporary wired Lisp definitions, recording the old entry, new entry, and reservation-counter change when their required reservation changes disagree.  Preserve normal return values and leave the counter unchanged.  Include allocation, release, flag changes, and page writeback.  Save this diagnostic image and inspect the first inconsistent transition after restore.  The instrumentation is a development fixture and will be removed after diagnosis.

The transition-audit fixture repeated the earlier package-reader mistake: it used SYS.INT before selecting MEZZANO.SUPERVISOR.  The reader stopped before changing any definition.  Inspected and released owned stop177, then added the initial IN-PACKAGE form.

The discrepancy comes from COUNT-BLOCK-RESERVATIONS counting entries with block ID zero.  MAP-SG-VEC-IN-PAGER writes flags #x43 (present, writable, transient) for physical mappings without allocating or reserving a disk block.  BLOCK-INFO-FOR-VIRTUAL-ADDRESS already treats block ID zero as having no backing block.  The new count included these entries because their committed bit is clear.  Existing transient entries inflated initialization, and a newly created boot mapping reduced the measured allowance by one.

A controlled one-page MAKE-DMA-BUFFER test reproduced the defect without reboot: counter minus computed reservations was 255 before mapping, 254 during mapping, and 255 after RELEASE-DMA-BUFFER.  Evidence: reservation-transient-before.log.  Recommendation: count only leaf entries with a nonzero block ID, then apply the existing lazy/uncommitted/wired rule.  Add a test requiring transient allocation and release to preserve the allowance.  Remove all temporary transition diagnostics, save the corrected definitions, and require boot initialization, full GC, repeated saves, and restoration to satisfy the exact 256-block allowance.  No live counter adjustment is required: the next boot will initialize it from the corrected count.

Review of INITIALIZE-PAGER's lock invariant found that the new ordinary-thread statistics reader must inhibit snapshots while holding *VM-LOCK*.  TAKE-SNAPSHOT stops ordinary threads before acquiring that lock.  Otherwise it could stop the reader while the reader held a lock needed by the snapshot thread.  Recommendation: wrap the wired statistics read in the existing WITH-SNAPSHOT-INHIBITED mechanism.  Test concurrent statistics sampling during snapshot completion, in addition to the post-restore count assertion.

## Snapshot retry timer lifetime: 2026-10-13

SCAVENGABLE-THREAD-P excludes :SUPERVISOR and :IDLE stacks.  Its stated invariant requires objects referenced there to have another live owner.  SNAPSHOT-THREAD removes a timer from the pool with WITH-TIMER and retains it only in its local variable between retries.  While disarmed, that timer has no independently traced owner.  Recommendation: keep one snapshot retry timer in a kernel global, initialize it during INITIALIZE-SNAPSHOT, and use it directly in SNAPSHOT-THREAD.  This follows the existing ownership of the snapshot disk request and bounce buffer.

The restored snapshot worker is initialized but has not executed until requested.  Hot loading its new definition therefore requires resetting that untouched worker through RESET-EPHEMERAL-THREAD, under the stopped-world lock, after asserting that it is sleeping with frame pointer zero.  Do not reset an executing worker.  Initialize the new timer global before that reset.  The next boot performs ordinary initialization.

INITIALIZE-SNAPSHOT also clears the completion event even though it resets *SNAPSHOT-IN-PROGRESS* to NIL.  Read-only inspection found restored WAIT-FOR-SNAPSHOT-COMPLETION callers still asleep after boot.  Set that event true during initialization so those callers can finish.  Tests must verify the initial completion state after restore, full collection of a disarmed retry timer without loss of the global timer, forced snapshot retry while inhibited, concurrent statistics reads during saves, and repeated restoration.

The first worker migration helper panicked with Not on wired stack before resetting the worker.  WITH-WORLD-STOPPED leaves its caller on the ordinary stack, while WITHOUT-INTERRUPTS requires a wired stack.  Replaced that form with SAFE-WITHOUT-INTERRUPTS after reading its definition, which changes stacks through the existing kernel mechanism.  Preserved the console as snapshot-worker-install-panic.log and repeated restoration from the saved image.

The retry's CHECK reported 518330 mapped reservations and counter 518600: an excess of 14 above the 256-block allowance.  The attempted retained debugger restore then found its saved handle NIL.  Image modification time 20:57:58 precedes fixture preparation at 21:00:49.  The successful snapshot logs also retain the old counting allowance of 255.  The previous claim that the completed image contained the corrected transient count and latest fixtures was wrong.  The subsequent timer panic prevented that newer save.  Current definitions have now been compiled and loaded, and the untouched snapshot worker reset successfully with the globally retained timer.  Prepare new fixtures and save these definitions before testing corrected boot accounting.  Do not adjust the live counter or describe absent fixtures as successful restores.

Its restore panicked in a previously saved test evaluation, d258f4debdd242a3ffa06340c359e8ec8b66d0bb62381b552783e2686f2cc882, when INSPECT-WAITER entered SAFE-WITHOUT-INTERRUPTS and accessed address 0x8002813008.  INSPECT-WAITER was loaded in pageable code (caller PC 0x80025e9ee3).  The supervisor restrictions prohibit pageable code and data with interrupts disabled.  Preserve snapshot-sync-inspector-panic.log and its QEMU core, recover the preceding image, and load the synchronization fixture with :WIRED T.  Add a residency precondition before disabling interrupts so incorrect loading signals an ordinary test error.  The completion-event correction allowed the old saved evaluation to resume and exposed this test defect.  The browser restore marker remained unset.

With the synchronization fixture compiled and loaded into wired memory, snapshot restoration passed.  The completion event was true and no snapshot was in progress. The second save passed 2419 concurrent reservation samples and retained the 256-block allowance before and after completion. ## Default Proceed selection: 2026-10-13

Reviewed [Genera proceeding and restarting](https://www.chai.uni-hamburg.de/~moeller/symbolics-info/documentation/Program-Development-Utilities.pdf#page=203) and [INVOKE-RESTART-INTERACTIVELY](https://www.lispworks.com/documentation/HyperSpec/Body/f_invo_2.htm), the captured restart list, command acceptance/replay, and the guest command parser.  Genera assigns RESUME to the innermost proceed handler, falling back to the innermost restart handler.  Common Lisp's restart objects do not carry that distinction.

Recommendation under standing authorization: implement `:Proceed` using the first captured restart named CL:CONTINUE.  When it is absent, reject the request with :NO-CONTINUE-RESTART before command acceptance and require an explicit choice.  `:Proceed N` invokes the zero-based captured restart N interactively through the existing operation.  `:Proceed Default` is equivalent to omitting the index.  Mark the selected default in the restart listing.  This mapping is a documented Common Lisp adaptation, and does not infer that an arbitrary restart is safe to select automatically.

Keep the accepted operation and its argument in the existing numbered command record.  Use the stopped thread's existing interactive reader and command abort boundary.  No structure layout, new transport, or dependency is needed.  Complete this increment before adding explicit Lisp argument forms, whose acceptance and replay require separate source-value handling.

Tests: typed default/index parsing and completion, innermost CONTINUE selection, rejection without a CONTINUE and without consuming a command ID, interactive input, normal return with multiple values, nonlocal exit, exact cleanup, same-ID replay after exit, and Listener reuse.  Chromium must exercise typed Proceed, the equivalent button, explicit choice, missing default, and successful evaluation afterward.

The first Proceed compile caught an extra closing parenthesis in the edited ASSISTANCE parse branch: HANDLER-CASE read the following LET* as a handler clause.  Inspected the compiler condition through the retained debugger, corrected the delimiter, and released owned stop182 and its job before recompiling.

The debugger service, Command Processor, and extended control fixture compiled in Mezzano at safety 3/debug 1/speed 1 with warnings NIL and failure NIL.  The fixture passed typed default/index plans and completion, nested Abort, first-CONTINUE selection among duplicate names, interactive arguments, returning multiple values, nonlocal continuation, same-ID replay without reinvocation, missing-default rejection without command-ID advancement, cleanup, and Listener reuse.  Existing service and debugger-interruption fixtures also passed.  Evidence: debugger-proceed-load-test.log and debugger-proceed-existing.log.

The Web test found that STOPCOMMANDPENDING counted a definitively rejected mutation receipt as pending.  A missing-default rejection therefore disabled further debugger commands.  The correction excludes rejected receipts from the busy predicate, retains their error and history, and continues to block unknown outcomes.  Rejection has no accepted command to poll.  The first SPACE-completion browser test omitted the key's text from its simulated event.  Correcting that test input produced the expected guest completion without a product change.

The corrected focused Chromium run passed 12 groups, including typed default/index Proceed, restart buttons, missing-default recovery, nested Abort and reload, interruption of an interactive reader, normal return of 81/:CONTINUED at the same stop, and subsequent Listener evaluation returning 42.  It released nine owned jobs, eleven stops, and its session.  The broad UI suite also passed and released its fixtures.  Fourteen isolated HTTP tests and JavaScript syntax checks passed.  Evidence: /tmp/listener-debugger-proceed-second/result.json and /tmp/listener-debugger-proceed-broad.log on the browser host.  Web test details and expected error responses are recorded in tools/swank/web/TESTING.md.

This increment was tested in the running x86-64 development guest.  Explicit Lisp argument expressions and the unchecked delivery items remain planned.  No SBCL process was used.

## Explicit restart arguments: 2026-10-13

Reviewed [INVOKE-RESTART](https://www.lispworks.com/documentation/HyperSpec/Body/f_invo_1.htm), [EVAL](https://www.lispworks.com/documentation/HyperSpec/Body/f_eval.htm), [LIST-LENGTH](https://www.lispworks.com/documentation/HyperSpec/Body/f_list_l.htm), the guest command keyword parser, retained QUERY-IO, and numbered command replay.  Required restart parameters currently depend on an application-supplied interactive function.

Recommendation under standing authorization: add `:Proceed N :Arguments`, with omitted N selecting the default CONTINUE.  The existing Boolean keyword convention makes an omitted :Arguments equivalent to No and a mentioned :Arguments without a value equivalent to Yes.  No retains interactive invocation.  Yes submits :INVOKE-RESTART-WITH-ARGUMENTS with NIL for the default or an integer index.  Resolve the restart before accepting the command, then prompt on QUERY-IO for one Lisp expression whose primary value is a finite proper argument list.  Evaluate on the stopped thread and apply INVOKE-RESTART.  Use LIST-LENGTH to reject circular lists before APPLY.  Ordinary type errors reject dotted lists and atoms.

Reading disables read-time evaluation.  EVAL uses the current dynamic environment and null lexical environment, as Common Lisp specifies.  Selected-frame lexical evaluation remains separate work.  The command's existing abort boundary handles reader, evaluator, argument, and restart errors.  Existing numbered commands and retained input offsets provide replay without evaluating an accepted command twice.  No new source payload, command representation, transport, or dependency is required.

Tests must cover required parameters without an interactive function, default and explicit indices, Boolean keyword defaults and completion, dynamic bindings, multiple values, cleanup, exact-once argument evaluation and restart invocation under replay, NIL arguments, dotted/circular/non-list results, read/evaluation errors, nested Abort, interruption while awaiting arguments, browser reload during that wait, and successful Listener evaluation afterward.  Preserve the stopped computation after aborting an argument command.

## Reservation reader and collection: 2026-10-13

Saving Proceed entered a stopped-world wait cycle before the new snapshot began.  Read-only host inspection found the reservation reader in COUNT-BLOCK-RESERVATIONS, holding the VM read lock.  The lock state was 5: one reader and a pending writer.  The Garbage Collector thread had stopped ordinary threads and requested ALLOCATE-MEMORY-RANGE.  The pager waited for the VM write lock in ALLOCATE-MEMORY-RANGE-IN-PAGER.  The suspended reader could not release the lock.  Current function headers confirmed the reader stack.  The initial host decoder used stale cold-image names and rejected adjustable symbol-name strings.  Correcting those diagnostics changed no guest memory.  Evidence: inspect-proceed-save.log, reservation-gc-stall.image, reservation-gc-stall.log, and reservation-gc-stall.core.539577.

The newly added BLOCK-RESERVATION-STATISTICS inhibited snapshots but allowed GC to stop its caller while holding the VM lock.  My earlier commentary placed the stall in catalog reload because no output had reached the host.  The stacks show the evaluation had advanced to the snapshot fixture and GC inside SNAPSHOT.  The catalog diagnosis was premature.

Recommendation: replace WITH-SNAPSHOT-INHIBITED with WITH-PSEUDO-ATOMIC around the resident statistics read.  WITH-WORLD-STOPPED, used by both GC and snapshot preparation, waits for pseudo-atomic readers.  The existing mechanism protects the complete lock interval with no pager protocol or new allocation.  No code or data under the VM lock may be pageable.  Recover from the preceding verified image, compile this definition into wired memory, and require concurrent statistics reads during repeated full collections, saves, and restoration.  Do not modify guest lock or scheduler state from the host.  Explicit restart arguments remain paused until this failure is corrected.

The corrected reader compiled into wired memory at safety 3/debug 1/speed 1 with warnings NIL and failure NIL.  Twelve full collections completed with eleven concurrent reservation samples.  The subsequent save passed three collections, four samples during collection, and 1927 samples during snapshot completion.  Restoration passed the saved completion waiter, expired debugger handles/controller, retained command outcome, locals after GC, CONTINUE and exact cleanup, timed condition-variable notification/timeout/mutex reacquisition, and three further collections with concurrent reads.  Every count retained the 256-block allowance.  The save's resumed prior-boot job reported twenty samples up to its saved point and completed without another save.  Evidence: reservation-reader-pa-load.log, reservation-reader-pa-test.log, reservation-reader-pa-save.log, and reservation-reader-pa-restore.log.  Preserved the corrected saved image as reservation-reader-pa-saved.image.  Tests ran on x86-64 QEMU with two CPUs and 2 GiB RAM.  ARM64 and cold-build execution remain untested.

## Guest-owned frame selection: 2026-10-13

Reviewed the retained service, command parser, structure redefinition machinery, browser selection state, and [Genera stack motion](https://www.chai.uni-hamburg.de/~moeller/symbolics-info/documentation/Program-Development-Utilities.pdf#page=220).  Current selection is browser-owned and passed back as an opaque frame token for each locals request.  This prevents another controller from discovering the selected frame.

Recommendation under standing authorization: store one current-frame index in each guest stop, initially zero.  Add :CURRENT-FRAME, :SELECT-FRAME, :NEXT-FRAME, :PREVIOUS-FRAME, :TOP-FRAME, and :BOTTOM-FRAME operations.  Selection and movement return the index, printed function, and existing local records.  Next increases the index toward callers.  Previous decreases it toward callees.  Top selects zero, and Bottom selects the last retained frame.  Next and Previous take a nonnegative count through :Nframes, defaulting to one.  A mentioned keyword requires its value.  Zero displays the current frame.  Reject an out-of-range target before acceptance, preserving selection and command ID.  The existing :LOCALS token operation remains available.

The target thread changes selection under the service mutex before printing outside that mutex.  A printer error can enter a child debugger, whose selection starts at zero and does not alter its parent.  Current-frame information becomes NIL after stop closure.  Replay uses the existing operation and EQL integer argument.  No transport representation or dependency is added.  The browser derives selection from guest stop information and command results and removes its saved selection state.  Find Frame, frame filtering, detailed argument/disassembly views, and lexical evaluation remain pending.

Mezzano supports structure redefinition and initializes added slots during instance migration.  Add the index as a trailing slot.  Test a stop created before redefinition, selection after full GC, controller replacement, nested printing failure and Abort, boundaries and zero movement, identical relative-movement replay, command parsing/completion, and closed-stop state.  Save a selected stop, restore it with expired old handles, inspect its selected locals, continue it, and require exact cleanup.  Chromium must test typed and mouse selection, movement, reload, reconnect, nested-stop independence, and subsequent Listener input.  Compile and execute guest tests in Mezzano.

The first compiled frame test caught a plan error before exercising the prepared stop: Show Local 2 retained the parser's positional argument as the current-frame service argument.  The browser no longer substitutes a frame token, so the plan must explicitly set :ARGUMENT NIL while retaining :LOCAL-INDEX 2.  Corrected that guest plan, inspected the retained assertion condition and frames, and released only the failed driver job.  The pre-redefinition fixture remains available for the retry.

The corrected compiled frame fixture passed on the stop created before structure redefinition.  It verified command parsing and keyword completion, default and zero movement, all stack boundaries, invalid arguments without command-ID advancement, identical relative-movement replay, controller replacement, full GC and caller locals, independent child selection during a printer failure, command Abort preserving parent selection, result 333/:FRAME-TEST, and one cleanup.  Evidence: debugger-frames-load-test.log and debugger-frames-retry.log.  Stop information now gates its current-frame field on :STOPPED so an older closed record migrated with the new slot's default cannot report a live selection.

The final guest service and restoration fixture compiled with warnings NIL and failure NIL.  The frame fixture passed again on a newly created stop.  Existing service, restart/control, and debugger-interruption fixtures also passed, including nested input, explicit arguments, replay, and cleanup.  Evidence: debugger-frames-existing.log.  Browser and selected-frame restoration tests remain pending.

Browser review found that inspecting whenever another controller changes the selected index can invoke its failing printer again after child Abort.  Recommendation: polling updates selection and renders a matching retained result when available, but does not submit an inspection solely because an external controller changed the frame.  The user can request :Show Frame.  Initial stop inspection and explicit movement retain their existing behavior.  This removes an automatic application-code invocation without adding browser state or scanning command history.  Test that external selection changes the highlight without advancing the command ID, then that explicit Show Frame obtains locals.  Retest the failed-printer Abort case.

Web source review also found that matching a returned frame result against the live index hides its printed locals when closure clears that index.  Preserve the last returned inspection for a closed stop, label it as a recorded frame from that closed stop, and keep its live selection and controls cleared.  Test the recorded local table after continuation.  This uses retained command results and grants no frame access after closure.

## Local-value availability diagnosis: 2026-10-13

Source review found that READ-FRAME-SLOT returns the legal keyword :$INACCESSIBLE-VALUE$ for unavailable storage.  LOCAL-VARIABLE-VALUE interprets any equal value as unavailable.  A compiled debug-3/safety-3 function whose VALUE argument holds that keyword reproduced the error: inspection returned (NIL NIL), while resumed execution returned :$INACCESSIBLE-VALUE$.  Evidence: debugger-local-availability-probe.log.  This remains unresolved while the frame-navigation browser and restoration tests finish.

The same reader wraps closure-path access in IGNORE-ERRORS, with an existing comment attributing failures to overwritten compiler environments.  Source review establishes that those errors are discarded, but does not establish whether that older compiler defect still occurs.  Review the reader's callers and closure tests before changing this path.  The next correction should return availability separately from the value and allow malformed accessible closure data to signal its error through the existing debugger boundary.  Missing storage and a failed read must remain distinct.

## Frame-navigation restoration failure: 2026-10-13

The save completed after three full collections and 2302 concurrent reservation samples.  Reservation counts retained the 256-block allowance.  Preserved the completed image as debugger-frames-saved.image.  Restart then failed before SWANK or the post-restore test driver ran.  The serial stack records an earlier bounds error in %FIND-HASH-TABLE-SLOT-1-STANDARD-EQ, called by the compiler's PREDICATE-INFO while compiling NIC-ADDED's format operation.  A second bounds error occurred in a weak EQL table during CLOS instance-update checking while formatting the earlier condition.  The native debugger then panicked because serial input is unimplemented.

Preserved the failed disk image, console, and QEMU core as debugger-frames-restore-panic.image, debugger-frames-restore-panic.log, and debugger-frames-restore-panic.core.552424. The compiler predicate table at 0x4800037cfc69 has count 30 and storage pointer 0x480009d7ed89.  The saved block-map entry for that storage page is 0x41a0227: present, writable, dirty-tracked, and zero-fill.  The restored page contains only zeros.  The saved map's zero-fill tail spans 0x480009c7b000 through 0x48000a000000.  The table page matches the saved disk bytes, including its pointer into that tail.  Evidence: inspect-frames-tables.log and inspect-frames-pages.log.  Disk bytes beneath a zero-fill entry are not saved object data.

Source review found that PROTECT-MEMORY-RANGE-IN-PAGER enables writes without checking an existing copy-on-write PTE.  Minor GC calls that path to disable dirty tracking on the old generation.  If snapshot writeback still owns the page, writes can bypass its copy fault and alter the pending saved copy.  This is a concrete protection invariant violation and a candidate explanation for the saved table referring to later heap allocation.

Recommendation: recover debugger-arguments-saved.image, reproduce the protection violation on an isolated page in compiled Mezzano, and preserve read-only protection whenever the PTE is copy-on-write.  Keep existing page copying and writeback mechanisms.  Test ordinary writable and dirty-tracked pages, pending snapshot protection, write-triggered copying, and a collection during snapshot writeback followed by restoration.  Do not edit saved heap contents, rebuild individual tables, or treat a successful retry as proof of a correction.

The first probe failed in test setup.  Its assertion assumed an empty snapshot writeback list after boot, but restored globals retained an obsolete physical-frame index and count 824 until the next TAKE-SNAPSHOT resets them.  Constructing the assertion condition on the pager thread caused a page fault in that thread.  Evidence: snapshot-cow-setup-panic.log and snapshot-cow-setup-panic-inspection.log.  The test did not reach the protection change.  Simplify the direct probe to set and restore the copy-on-write bit on its private unused stack page, inspect protection flags, and perform assertions on the ordinary evaluation thread.  Test data preservation through a real save separately.

The simplified compiled probe reproduced the violation without changing guest data: ordinary writable protection was 2, dirty-tracked protection was 0, and copy-on-write protection changed from 1024 to 1026.  Bit 1024 marks copy-on-write and bit 2 permits writes.  Evidence: snapshot-cow-probe-second.log.  Add the copy-on-write check to the existing writable branch.  The integration test holds the real snapshot worker immediately before writeback, forces a minor collection, changes all 8192 fixture values from 1111 to 2222, and releases writeback.  Restoring that image must recover 1111 in every element.  The saved debugger stop and timed synchronization fixtures run in the same image.

The corrected protection probe passed: ordinary writable 2, dirty-tracked 0, and copy-on-write 1024 before and after the protection change.  The real snapshot test forced a minor collection with 80569 pages pending, changed the live vector to 2222, completed the save, and restored all 8192 values as 1111.  Evidence: snapshot-cow-correction.log, snapshot-cow-save.log, and snapshot-cow-restore.log.

Frame selection now occupies that ID, so acceptance correctly returned REQUEST-CONFLICT before inspecting the stale argument.  Inspected the condition and stack through the retained debugger.  Change the fixture to use NEXT-COMMAND-ID for that expired-frame request, preserving the intended validation test.  The restored application stop remains available.

After correcting the fixture's command ID, the restored selected frame passed expired handles/controller, retained command result, selected locals 111/222 after full GC, continuation returning 333/:RESTORED, and exact cleanup.  Timed condition-variable notification, timeout, mutex reacquisition, and cleanup passed.  The copy-on-write protection test and three full collections with concurrent reservation reads passed after boot, with the exact 256-block allowance.  Evidence: snapshot-cow-restore-retry.log. Existing compiled binding, binding-write, closure-write, SWANK local identity, frameless interruption with moving GC, and retained frame-navigation tests passed.  Evidence: debugger-local-availability-retry.log and debugger-local-existing-tests.log.  The test's register records are synthetic.  This does not add interrupted-register recovery, and ARM64 callee-save tracking remains untested.

The focused Chromium test passed seventeen groups, including available NIL and keyword values in the named compiled function, captured through a reader closure.  Continuation returned both values, and the following Listener expression returned 42.  All fifteen owned jobs, nineteen stops, and the session were released.  Web test commit 2f196d68 records the evidence and the first test fixture's anonymous frame-name mismatch.

After QEMU restart, the availability fixture, selected-frame restoration after full GC, expired handles/controller, retained command result, continuation and cleanup, and frameless interruption with moving GC all passed. ## Preserve local-function bindings: 2026-10-13

Reviewed Genera's [evaluation environment](https://www.chai.uni-hamburg.de/~moeller/symbolics-info/documentation/Program-Development-Utilities.pdf#page=203), Mezzano's first compiler pass, variable copying, closure lowering, register allocation, debug encoding, and both architecture code generators.  Frame evaluation must resolve FLET and LABELS bindings in the function namespace.  Current FROB-FLET-FUNCTION creates a lexical variable without the debug preservation flag.  A compiled debug-3/safety-3 function with an unused local helper exposed only VALUE and INSPECT in its frame.  Evidence: debugger-local-function-probe.log.

Recommendation: apply the existing debug-3 binding preservation to local-function bindings, using the policy where the function is introduced.  Retain a debug-name property when copying compiler variables.  Encode the debug name as (FUNCTION name), including (FUNCTION (SETF name)), through the existing name-object field in precise and closure metadata.  Ordinary variable names remain symbols.  This distinguishes both namespaces without changing the byte encoding or adding runtime environment objects.  Existing compiler passes already retain preserved bindings.  Local functions remain subject to their source declarations and closure allocation rules.

Compile the changed compiler in Mezzano.  Test unused FLET and recursive LABELS functions, a variable and function with the same name, nested shadowed functions, SETF function names, captured values, full GC, and mixed debug policies.  Invoke recovered function objects while the frame remains active and verify resumed results.  Rerun existing variable/closure preservation tests.  Chromium must display the function binding in a compiled stopped frame, inspect its captured ordinary value, continue successfully, and evaluate another Listener expression.  Save and restore the compiled compiler and a stopped test frame.  ARM64 code generation will be updated but execution remains untested.

This is a compiler prerequisite for lexical frame evaluation.  It does not preserve symbol-macro definitions or establish full lexical scope at every instruction, and it adds no remote evaluation operation.  Those requirements remain in the debugger plan.

The debug compressor and constant pool compare name objects with EQL.  Store the compound name once on the compiler variable and retain that same object when copying it.  Reconstructing a fresh list for each layout would add repeated metadata and constant-pool entries.  The name representation and byte encoding remain as specified above.

## Compiler-load stall: 2026-10-13

The local-function load driver compiled AST.LISP but stopped reporting before its COMPILED line.  A fresh retained-session attach timed out after 25 seconds.  Paused QEMU PID 556983 and inspected its memory read-only.  A SWANK control thread was waiting on Garbage Collector Cvar in %GC while allocating a thread stack.  The collector's saved state was ACTIVE with an older MARK-PINNED-OBJECT frame.  This capture does not establish the active CPU's current instruction or the cause of the stall.  No changed compiler definitions or local-function test have been confirmed loaded.

Preserved debugger-local-function-stall.core.556983, debugger-local-function-stall.image, and debugger-local-function-stall.serial.log before attempting host CPU-register queries.  Querying KVM registers from a separate process returned EIO.  A GDB call within QEMU was interrupted by SIGSTOP.  A second attempt received SIGCONT, and QEMU exited after detachment from the abandoned call.  That exit resulted from the host diagnostic attempt and is not evidence of a guest panic.

Recommendation: restore the verified debugger-local-saved.image, finish its saved test fixture, and add QEMU's existing GDB endpoint on host loopback port 1235 for direct register and memory inspection.  Reproduce the compiler-load stall before selecting a guest correction.  Do not disable GC, load a reduced substitute file, or treat a retry as a correction.

The repeated load reported a transient SWANK NO-APPLICABLE-METHOD for the compiler TEST accessor on an AST-IF instance.  Source review found that STD-ADD-METHOD removes and finalizes the old method before installing its replacement.  The class load nevertheless continued.  Subsequent retained-debugger inspection identified END-OF-FILE in TEST-DEBUG-LOCAL-FUNCTIONS.LISP, after all four changed compiler files had loaded.  The fixture had an unclosed closure-inspection helper.  Correct its delimiter and rerun the fixture.  Keep the transient accessor failure separate: it requires a method-replacement test and correction before repeating the complete class reload.

## CLOS replacement investigation: 2026-10-13

The [MOP method protocol](https://franz.com/support/documentation/mop/dictionary.html#add-method) requires calling REMOVE-METHOD before associating a replacement.  Publishing the replacement before that call would change observable protocol ordering.  No such reordering has been implemented.  Mezzano currently has no synchronization between STD-ADD-METHOD's removal/addition and dispatch cache construction.

Prepare a deterministic compiled probe with an isolated generic function and a dependent observer.  On removal, the observer releases a waiting reader thread and waits for its result before permitting replacement to finish.  The reader catches and reports the condition as test evidence.  Verify the old result before replacement and the new result afterward.  This establishes whether the method-free interval is observable independently of compiler reload and transport timing.  Synchronization design remains under review.  It must preserve MOP ordering, prevent stale cache publication, and release any lookup lock before executing an ordinary application method.

The deterministic probe compiled without warnings or failures and reproduced NO-APPLICABLE-METHOD on the reader thread during the dependent's removal notification.  Calls before and after replacement returned :OLD and :NEW.  Evidence: clos-replacement-probe-second.log.  The first driver incorrectly named a host file as a guest LOCAL pathname.  Its file-not-found stop was inspected and released before correcting the source location to SYS:SOURCE.

The local-function compiler fixture passed unused FLET, recursive LABELS, same-name variable/function bindings, nested function shadowing, SETF function names, closure metadata, full GC, and mixed debug policies. Evidence: debugger-local-function-test-retry.log and debugger-local-function-existing-tests.log.  Chromium passed eighteen focused groups, including displayed local-function and ordinary-variable bindings, continuation, and subsequent Listener input.  Web evidence is recorded in c5df4eed.

The compiler increment remains independent of the open CLOS replacement correction.  Add a compiled restoration fixture with a selected condition-stopped FLET frame containing HELPER=5 and a separate local HELPER function capturing 37.  Inspect both bindings after full GC before saving and after restoring.  Proceed must return 42/:LOCAL-FUNCTION-RESTORED and perform exactly one cleanup.  Rerun the compiler fixture after restoration to test the saved compiler definitions.

The first restoration setup asserted unqualified printed binding names while the worker's package was CL-USER.  Retained inspection showed the correct available bindings with package-qualified names.  Bind the fixture worker's package explicitly.  Also corrected the fixture's Proceed check to require :UNWOUND instead of using the helper that requires :RETURNED.  Released the driver stop, inspected and continued its application fixture, and verified 42/:LOCAL-FUNCTION-RESTORED plus one cleanup.  The corrected setup compiled without warnings or failures, inspected the expected bindings, and completed its snapshot.  Evidence: debugger-local-function-save-inspection.log, debugger-local-function-cleanup.log, and debugger-local-function-save-retry.log.

The completed image was preserved as debugger-local-functions-saved.image and restarted with the existing QEMU arguments.  The restored selected frame retained available HELPER=5 and (FUNCTION HELPER) after full GC.  Proceed returned 42/:LOCAL-FUNCTION-RESTORED and executed one cleanup.  The local-function compiler fixture and the local-value availability fixture then passed using the saved compiler definitions.  Evidence: debugger-local-functions-restore.log.  These tests cover x86-64 QEMU with two CPUs and 2 GiB RAM, debug-3/safety-3/speed-1 fixture functions, and a debug-1/safety-3/speed-1 compiler.  ARM64 execution and a cold image build remain untested.  Post-restore browser tests are separate work.  Concurrent CLOS method replacement remains a reproduced open defect.

## CLOS replacement synchronization: 2026-10-13

Recommendation under standing authorization: associate one supervisor mutex with each generic function through an EQ weak-key synchronized hash table.  Install a newly allocated mutex with the existing CAS GETHASH operation so concurrent first users share one mutex.  The mutex name must not retain its generic-function key.  This uses existing runtime mechanisms and avoids a change to bootstrap metaobject layouts.  A single global method lock would also block unrelated generic-function cache misses while a MOP hook waits or enters the debugger.

Hold the generic function's mutex during ADD-METHOD, REMOVE-METHOD, finalization, cache invalidation, and slow dispatch construction.  Permit recursive entry by its owning thread because the MOP requires REMOVE-METHOD and may call the generic function during notifications.  Preserve the specified removal, association, specializer backpointer, discriminator, and dependent-notification sequence.  Concurrent callers may finish an already selected old method.  They must not construct or publish dispatch from the temporary missing-method state.  Return the selected effective function from the locked lookup and invoke it after releasing the mutex.  Cached calls keep their existing path.

Test deterministic overlap during the removal notification, cache-miss publication, same-thread MOP reentry, old/new results, method backpointers and notification order, normal removal, and errors releasing the mutex.  Test reader/writer, ordinary single dispatch, EQL dispatch, multiple dispatch, and unspecialized methods.  An application method must be able to wait for another thread to replace its method, proving that ordinary invocation does not retain the update lock.  Repeat the complete compiler reload while retained requests run.  Compile and execute in Mezzano, then rerun debugger tests and image restoration.  Class-slot-layout mutation and simultaneous mutation of different mutually dependent generic functions require separate concurrency review.

The complete revised CLOSETTE.LISP compiled in Mezzano: 486 functions, warnings NIL, failure NIL.  Loaded that compiled file, then compiled the replacement fixture without warnings or failures.  Ordinary, unspecialized, and multiple-dispatch overlap cases passed before the EQL case failed its specializer-backpointer assertion.  Retained inspection showed that the removed EQL method had METHOD-GENERIC-FUNCTION NIL but remained in SPECIALIZER-DIRECT-METHODS.  Evidence: clos-replacement-compile.log, clos-replacement-load.log, and clos-replacement-load-inspection.log.

Source diagnosis: SAFE-REMOVE-DIRECT-METHOD calls ADD-DIRECT-METHOD in its non-class-specializer branch.  The existing REMOVE-DIRECT-METHOD method for EQL-SPECIALIZER performs the required removal.  Recommendation: correct that single call, rerun every dispatch case, and verify the old method disappears from both the generic function and its specializers.  This corrects the removal operation rather than filtering stale records during inspection.

After correcting EQL removal, all replacement cases passed: ordinary, unspecialized, multiple, and EQL dispatch, readers/writers, MOP ordering/backpointers, mutex release on errors, normal removal, and a running application method waiting for replacement by another thread.  The complete compiler reload then passed under retained SWANK polling, followed by the local-function fixture.  Evidence: clos-replacement-reload.log and clos-compiler-reload.log.  Used the failed test's exact retained frame to release its old EQL backpointer and attached replacement, then released its stop/job/session.  Evidence: clos-replacement-cleanup.log.

Further cache review found that an already selected discriminator retains an earlier cache, but a cache miss writes into CLASSES-TO-EMF-TABLE's current cache.  The cache may now use a different representation.  A compiled probe captured an ordinary discriminator, replaced its method with a slot reader, initialized the new reader cache, invoked the old discriminator, then invoked the current reader.  The last call received a closure where FAST-SLOT-READ requires an integer slot location.  Evidence: clos-stale-dispatch-probe-result.log.  The probe's first draft had an unmatched delimiter, then returned its cleanup value because PROGN ended in the wrong place.  Both fixture errors were corrected before obtaining that result.

Recommendation: pass the originating cache to each slow dispatch entry.  Under the generic-function mutex, build an entry only if that cache is still current.  Otherwise return the generic function for redispatch after releasing the mutex.  Earlier compiled discriminators that do not supply a cache enter a fresh initial discriminator.  When initial dispatch changes between slot locations and effective functions, clear the old cache and allocate a new one instead of reusing its storage.  Cache identity then identifies both the method definition and entry representation.  Remove the existing branch that silently clears a non-function effective-method entry.  Such an entry must signal a type error.

Test old discriminators across ordinary/reader replacement, heterogeneous reader/ordinary methods, and single/multiple-dispatch transitions.  Add a cache-construction overlap test in which replacement waits until an old lookup finishes publishing its entry.  These tests supplement the removal-interval fixture.

The cache-identity draft failed to read because the generated one-argument discriminator had one extra closing delimiter.  I started the expanded fixture before checking that compile result, so it ran against the preceding loaded implementation and reproduced the stale reader-cache type error.  Inspected both retained conditions, corrected the delimiter, and released both failed jobs.  These runs did not validate the cache-identity correction.  Evidence: clos-cache-reload-inspection.log and clos-cache-test-inspection.log.

The corrected cache-identity implementation compiled all 486 CLOS functions with warnings NIL and failure NIL.  The expanded fixture passed the removal interval, MOP ordering/backpointers, ordinary/EQL/unspecialized/multiple dispatch, reader/writer replacement, ordinary-to-reader replacement with an old discriminator, heterogeneous reader/ordinary methods, single/multiple-dispatch transitions, cache-construction overlap, full GC with stable lock identity, error unwinding, normal removal, and an application method waiting for concurrent replacement.  Evidence: clos-cache-reload-retry.log.  The existing non-function-entry recovery branch now signals TYPE-ERROR instead of clearing the cache.  Integration, browser, and restoration tests for this final version remain pending.

The final implementation passed a complete AST/PASS1/LOWER-ENVIRONMENT/x86-64 CODEGEN reload while retained requests continued, then the local-function, binding-preservation, binding-write, closure-write, SWANK local-identity, frameless-interruption, frame-navigation, value-availability, and CLOS concurrency fixtures.  Evidence: clos-debugger-integration.log.  Save this compiled implementation with both the selected local-function frame and the existing selected-frame restoration fixture.  After restart, finish both stops, rerun CLOS concurrency and local-function compilation, and repeat fresh frameless interruption.  Browser acceptance follows that restoration.

The snapshot completed and was preserved as clos-debugger-saved.image.  After restarting QEMU from that image, both saved stops retained their selected frames and values after full GC, continued successfully, and ran their cleanup exactly once.  The CLOS concurrency fixture, local-function compiler fixture, and fresh frameless-interruption fixture also passed.  Evidence: clos-debugger-save.log, clos-debugger-saved.serial.log, and clos-debugger-restore.log.  Tests used x86-64 QEMU with two CPUs and 2 GiB RAM.  Fixture functions used debug 3/safety 3/speed 1.  ARM64 execution and a cold image build remain untested.  Browser acceptance is running separately.

Follow-up source review: a lookup MOP callback can recursively replace methods on the same generic function while its thread owns the mutex.  Several lookup helpers obtain the current cache again after calling that callback.  The existing concurrency fixtures test replacement from another thread and metadata inspection in a removal callback.  They do not test this same-thread mutation.  Recommendation: reproduce the case with an isolated generic-function subclass before selecting a correction.  Check that calls after replacement retain the replacement result and that a callback cannot publish an older method into the replacement cache.  Preserve the completed concurrent-replacement evidence separately.

## Recursive method replacement during lookup: 2026-10-14

The isolated compiled probe replaced an INTEGER/INTEGER method from COMPUTE-EFFECTIVE-METHOD-FUNCTION on the same thread.  The removed method's owner became NIL and the replacement belonged to the generic function, but the first, second, and third calls all returned :OLD.  The first call had already selected the old method.  The following calls read the old effective function published in the new cache.  Evidence: clos-recursive-probe.log.

Recommendation: retain each lookup's original cache and publish its result only while that cache remains current.  Check cache identity again after constructing an initial discriminator, because applicability and method-combination callbacks can replace methods during construction.  For unspecialized dispatch, which has no cache object, retain the original method list and install the computed function only while that list is current.  The in-progress call may complete its selected old method.  Later calls must use the replacement.  These checks use existing cache and method-list identities and add no generation counter or object field.

Compile the complete CLOS source in Mezzano.  Test recursive replacement in applicability, effective-function construction, and a long-form method combination, including a recursive call that initializes the replacement cache.  Cover single, multiple, EQL, and unspecialized dispatch, then repeat the concurrent-replacement fixture.  Run the compiler/debugger integration and save/restore tests after this correction.  Browser tests must finish or pause before loading changed runtime definitions.

The corrected CLOSETTE.LISP compiled all 486 functions without warnings or failure.  The 34-function fixture compiled without warnings or failure and passed concurrent replacement plus seven recursive-replacement cases.  Recursive cases cover applicability, effective-function construction, and long-form method combination across multiple, single, EQL, and unspecialized dispatch.  Each callback makes a recursive call that initializes the replacement before returning to the pending lookup.  The original probe now returns (:FIRST :OLD :SECOND :NEW :THIRD :NEW).  Evidence: clos-recursive-reload.log.  Compiler/debugger integration and image-restoration tests are running separately.

The compiler reload and debugger integration passed with the recursive correction.  Saved the compiled implementation and two selected debugger stops in clos-recursive-saved.image after the serial log reported End snapshot.  After restarting that image, both selected stops retained their locals through full GC, continued, and performed one cleanup.  CLOS concurrency and recursive replacement, local-function compilation, and fresh frameless interruption also passed.  Evidence: clos-recursive-integration.log, clos-recursive-save.log, clos-recursive-saved.serial.log, and clos-recursive-restore.log.  This is x86-64 QEMU evidence.  ARM64 execution, a cold build, class-layout changes, and concurrent shared-specializer metadata changes by different generic functions remain outside these tests.

The first post-restore Web run on the preceding CLOS increment completed its eighteen behavior groups but recorded one unexpected HTTP 409 beyond its three intended rejections.  Its test driver had not retained failed request bodies.  The diagnostic rerun retained those bodies and passed with only the intended rejections.  No cause or correction for the fourth response has been established.  The Web agent is repeating the lost-reply FINISH/next-evaluation transition on the current saved image with exact failure capture and unchanged acceptance checks.  The Web journal records those results separately.
